It always did; the cure is a submission ask that says whether it came with an event, so the model validates that one | Compatibility | XS | a classic-mode page relying on `requestSubmit()` to refuse an invalid form | activate.c `submit`, libdom window.c `submit` |
| **A date past a Date's range is kept, and judged by nothing** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P4b's cold read). A date, month, week or local date and time: the value sanitizer reads its year as the residue mod 400, so it keeps `300000-01-01`; the judge reads years only through 275760 (`value.c`'s `whole_year`: past 2^53 ms, in the 287,000s, a double no longer holds every millisecond a local date and time's step needs), so such a value has no number, and its min, max and step say nothing. Chrome's sanitizer empties it. **Cure:** the sanitizer refuses what does not convert, which `valueAsNumber` wants too | Compatibility | XS | a page relying on max to refuse a far-future date | `value.c` `valid_for` / `whole_year` |
| **A whole form's validation walks the document once per radio** (booked 2026-10-28, Codex on 0f's P4a). A node's radio-group answer (`p_radio_group_missing`) walks its tree, which a single `validity` read can afford; a form's `checkValidity()` and a person's submission ask it of every radio, so a form of R radios costs R walks. Codex measured, on a sanitized host: 1,000 radios 0.38 s, 2,000 1.57 s, 4,000 6.27 s. The model's own validation shares its group chains and stays linear. **Cure:** one walk per validation pass, every group's answer kept until the tree or the state changes | Perf | S | a form with hundreds of radio groups | `state.c` `p_radio_group_missing`, `validity.c` `d_form_validate` |
| **`url`'s typeMismatch is a scheme check, not the URL parser** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P4a). A value is a valid absolute URL here when it has a scheme and its `:`, a host after any slashes for the schemes that need one, and no space or control byte. WHATWG's parser refuses more (a bad port, a bad IPv6 literal, a host with forbidden code points); `os64/url.h`'s refuses too much (userinfo, IPv6, any scheme without `//`) to stand in for it | Compatibility | XS | a page that relies on a URL field refusing a malformed host | `submit.c` `url_valid` |
| **An SVG or MathML `a` is not a link.** The walk descends INTO foreign subtrees, so HTML nested in a `foreignObject` is seen normally; what is passed over is a foreign element itself, which is not an HTML link however it is spelled. No text face draws SVG, and SVG 2's `href` plus the legacy `xlink:href` are two spellings to read when one does | Feature | XS | the graphical browser, which draws SVG | `core.c` `collect_model` |
| **`os64_page_rebuild` — paid by D3.** A candidate model shares document-owned node state and pins its snapshot; an incomplete candidate returns NULL without changing the old state or publication. Detached control values and option identity survive rebuilt models. D5b integrates the rebuild loop and preserves widgets by node | Paid | S | [D3 PR #211](https://github.com/VBWizard/os64/pull/211) | LIBPAGE.md § Control state and allocation failure; DOM.md § D3, as built |
| **Control mutation history — implemented by D5a.** State-aware attribute/tree entrances observe input type/constraint/group changes, option insertion/removal and select `size`/`multiple` transitions without an intervening getter. They release explicit-empty selectedness when required, stage control effects and commit atomic HTML mutations, including dirty-text transfer to the value attribute. Refusal preserves tree/state revisions, borrowed values and accounting. libdom routes mutations through these entrances | Paid | M | D5b seats libdom in Yonder | DOM.md § D5a, as built; `userland/libpage/state.c`; `userland/libdom/LIBDOM.md` |
| **A page whose only redirect is a SCRIPT is still a blank page.** DuckDuckGo's click logger offers both — `window.parent.location.replace(...)` for a browser that runs script and a `meta` refresh in `noscript` for one that does not — and family K reaches the second. A site that offers only the first cannot be followed, and what a reader sees is an accurate sentence about a page with nothing in it. That is JavaScript's row, not this library's: LIBPAGE.md ruling 2 puts it after the graphical browser, and nothing in family K makes that day worse | Feature | L | JavaScript, per LIBPAGE.md ruling 2 | LIBPAGE.md § K |
| **Cookie storage and referrer policy belong to the navigator.** libfetch provides the per-hop wire hooks; libpage does not own session state | Feature | M | the first site that needs a session | LIBFETCH.md § Booked |

## HTML parsing compatibility

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **libhtml retains the historical select insertion rules.** In a select fragment, `<div>x<option>a<option>b` drops the div wrapper; current WHATWG customizable-select parsing and Chrome preserve it. D2b keeps D2a document behavior and the pinned reference corpus unchanged. Migrating the document and fragment rules together needs current reference expectations and consumer validation | Compatibility | M | a page needs modern select content, or the approved corpus is migrated | `userland/libhtml/tree.c` (`reset_mode`, `M_SELECT`); LIBHTML.md § Proof; DOM.md § D2b, as built |

## Browser document lifetime

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Node collection beyond D6.** Unheld removed subtrees are reclaimed; binding wrappers and persistent page-state records retain strong holds until teardown. Created/cloned/failed-staged nodes never inserted remain charged. Permanent parser/creation names and original attributes stay in document chunks even when their node bodies are recycled. A weak wrapper table and collector-driven reachability require a separate design; D6's flat packed-fragment churn proof does not cover arbitrary create-and-drop workloads | Capacity | M | a page needs sustained create-and-drop or wrapper collection | `userland/libhtml/dom.c`, `core.c`; DOM.md § D6, as built and § Booked |

| **The arriving page is not shown until it is whole.** D4 keeps the old page on screen while the new one streams in (live until the new one's parse begins, when it is unloaded and its scripts stop); drawing the half-parsed tree is its own slice with its own proof (what a reader sees, when the old page goes, what a click on a box that is still moving does) | Feature | M | a page slow enough that a blank wait is worse than a growing one | yonder.c `g.stream` / DOM_D4.md § Booked |
| **The stream's slice is a byte budget (64 KiB).** The window's clock is the 10 ms tick, so bytes were chosen over time, and what one slice costs in the guest is recorded in DOM.md § D4, as built. A page whose bytes parse at very unequal speed would want a time budget off `micros()` | Performance | XS | the first page whose slices visibly hitch the window | yonder.c `STREAM_SLICE_BYTES` |

## Classic browser scripting

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A script app that keeps running can empty the page it was served** (booked 2026-10-30 from the P5). A code-hosting site's listing page shows its server-rendered content and then, a moment later, nothing, on some loads and not others: the deciding script either fails to fetch or runs out the 5 s budget (the page then runs without script and keeps its HTML), or it runs, starts the site's client-side app, and the app renders again without what it reaches for (`requestAnimationFrame`, `IntersectionObserver`, both MISSING on its records) and is left empty. Two pairs of P5 records show it, each pair one page loaded twice. Timing decides it, so a reload "fixes" it. **Cure:** the missing APIs — `requestAnimationFrame` (slice 5d, RELAYOUT.md) and `IntersectionObserver` (INTERSECTION_OBSERVER.md) both exist now; what else the app reaches for is a census of new records | Compatibility | M | a census of the app's new records | libdom (the missing interfaces); yonder `scripts.c` (the budget that hides it) |
| **A person's edit and key say less than a browser's** (booked 2026-10-29, 0f's P8). yonder tells the binding that a text control was edited, not how, so its `input` is an InputEvent whose `data` is null and `inputType` ""; a key's `code` is "" and its `location` 0, and a keypress is the only key with a `charCode`. A button or box clicked from the keyboard says `detail` 1, as a mouse's click does, because libui's activation does not say which it was. **Cure:** the host's event record carries the edit (libui's text field knows it), the key's position and the activation's source | Fidelity | S each | an editor that reads inputType, a game that reads `code`, a page that tells a keyboard's click by `detail` 0 | `dom.h` `os64_dom_event_t`, yonder `yonder.c` (`kind_event`, the key path), libos64 `ui.c` |
| **A MouseEvent a script makes has no scroll in its pageX.** CSSOM View adds the view's scroll offset to clientX outside a dispatch; here a constructed or `initMouseEvent`'d event's pageX and pageY are its clientX and clientY. `offsetX`/`offsetY`, `movementX`/`movementY` and `layerX`/`layerY` are absent, on host events too. C6e native validation of Curran’s 2D Wave Simulation reaches this: clicking the grid produces invalid cell coordinates and an exception, while hover-started animation works | Fidelity | S | a page that reads a synthetic event's pageX on a scrolled page, or offsetX at all (a drawing canvas) | libdom `event.c` (`ui_construct`, `ui_init`) |
| **A link a script follows before its page is shown goes nowhere** (booked 2026-10-29, 0f's P8's oracle). yonder performs a still-arriving page's follow and submit asks not at all (`navigation_perform`, `if (shown)`), so a link clicked or a form submitted by script during the parse does nothing; a `javascript:` link's code does not run. WPT's `Event-dispatch-click.html` clicks one and waits for it, and the page times out whole. **Cure:** the not-shown page's follow through the door an address ask takes (`page_of_host`) | Fidelity | S | a page that clicks a link or submits a form from an inline script | yonder `yonder.c` (`navigation_perform`'s FOLLOW and SUBMIT) |
| **The location is an ordinary object** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P7's oracle). HTML's Location is exotic: `Object.preventExtensions(location)` throws and its prototype is immutable (`Object.setPrototypeOf(location, {})` throws), and the cross-origin checks behind both. Here both succeed; `ancestorOrigins`, one of its [LegacyUnforgeable] members, is absent. WPT's `location-prevent-extensions.html` and `location-prototype-setting-same-origin.html` ask it. **Cure:** a location class with the two internal methods (QuickJS's exotic methods), and ancestorOrigins as a DOMStringList | Fidelity | S | a page or library that freezes or reparents the location | libdom `window.c` (the location object) |
| ~~**Navigator has four of its members**~~ **PAID 2026-11-04** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P7; GITHUB_WALLS.md Slice B): with the switch the navigator has NavigatorID's constants, `language` and `languages` (libjs's `OS64_JS_LOCALE`), `onLine`, `cookieEnabled` (the cookie provider's new `enabled`, which yonder answers from its jar), `hardwareConcurrency`, `maxTouchPoints`, and the plugin members over an empty `PluginArray` and `MimeTypeArray` (LIBDOM.md § The Modern Web's surface). What is left: `taintEnabled()` and `oscpu`, which HTML exposes only in Gecko's compatibility mode, are absent in it too, so a page under the Firefox agent finds neither (productSub and vendor do follow the mode); the empty lists are ordinary objects, so `navigator.plugins[0] = x` defines an index where WebIDL's legacy platform object refuses one (a strict-mode TypeError), though `length` stays 0; `registerProtocolHandler` and `unregisterProtocolHandler` (3 hosts of 191 in the 11/04 corpus count); the `Plugin` and `MimeType` interfaces, which no list here can ever hold one of; and `doNotTrack`, which no standard defines any longer | Compatibility | XS each | a page that needs one of them | libdom `classic.c`, `window.c` |
| **A collection's own property names list its indices, not its names** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P6b's oracle). An HTMLCollection answers `rows.foo` for a row whose id or name is foo, but `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(rows)` lists only `0` to `length - 1`; WebIDL's supported property names (unenumerable, as HTMLCollection's LegacyUnenumerableNamedProperties makes them) belong in it too. WPT's `table-rows.html` asks it of each table collection, and every collection shares the answer | Correctness | XS | a page or test that lists a collection's keys | libdom `collection.c` (the exotic's own keys) |
| **An area has no `relList`, and neither an area nor an anchor has the rest of its link members** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P6a). Promoted 2026-11-06: the Quick wins row "An anchor has no `hostname`…" carries the priority and the census. An area's `rel` reflects, but its `relList` would answer `supports()` with a link's tokens (stylesheet, modulepreload, preload) where a hyperlink's are noopener, noreferrer and opener; an anchor has only `href` of HTMLAnchorElement's members and HTMLHyperlinkElementUtils' (`origin`, `host`, `pathname` and the rest), and an area only its `href`. **Cure:** the hyperlink members once, on both, with a hyperlink's supported tokens | Feature | S | a page that reads a link's parts, or a slice for links | libdom `mixin.c` (R_REL_LIST, `rel_supported`), `elements.c` |
| **A style element has no `disabled`, and its sheet is never disabled** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P6a). `CSSStyleSheet.disabled` reads false and cannot be set (`sheets.c`), so HTMLStyleElement's `disabled`, which is that flag, is left out rather than made a setter that does nothing. **Cure:** a disabled flag the cascade skips a sheet for, with both members over it | Feature | S | a page that toggles a theme by disabling a sheet | libdom `sheets.c`; libpage's cascade |
| **A form control's shared reflection rows check no brand** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P6a's build). `controls.c`'s rows name every interface a member is on (`disabled` on input, textarea, select, button and fieldset), and its check asks the row, not the accessor's interface: HTMLInputElement's `disabled` getter called on a select answers the select's. WebIDL makes it a TypeError. P6a's `elements.c` gives each interface its own row for this reason. **Cure:** the same, one row per member and interface | Correctness | XS | a page or test that borrows one interface's accessor for another's element | libdom `controls.c` |
| **A template has none of declarative shadow DOM's members** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P6a). `shadowRootMode`, `shadowRootDelegatesFocus`, `shadowRootClonable`, `shadowRootSerializable` and the rest reflect attributes that attach a shadow root as the page parses, which yonder does not have. **Cure:** with shadow DOM | Feature | S | shadow DOM | libdom `elements.c` |
| **`:invalid` and `:valid` are not selectors** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P4a). A control's validity is judged (`validity`, libpage's judge), but the cascade's selector engine does not know the two pseudo-classes, so a page's `input:invalid { border-color: red }` styles nothing, and `querySelector(':invalid')` throws. Three of WPT's constraint pages ask it | Feature | S | a page that marks its invalid fields with CSS | libgarb's selector table |
| **An image button a person presses fires neither `submit` nor `invalid`** (booked 2026-10-28, 0f's P4a). yonder sends the form straight from the click (`IN_CLICK`), so its listeners never hear the submission and a script-judged `pattern` is not judged; the model's validation still runs. A button or Enter fires both (`page_validates`, then `submit`) | Compatibility | XS | a page whose image button's form listens for submit | yonder.c's `IN_CLICK` |
| Detached `new Image()` sources load when displayed, rather than prefetching. Chris accepted the first-hover fetch delay for D11 on 2026-10-06. Native cache jobs and detached-image load/error delivery need their own lifetime contract | Performance / feature | M | the next image-loading slice, or a real rollover whose first-hover delay matters | [DOM_D11.md](docs/design/pending/DOM_D11.md), libdom `classic.c`, yonder picture jobs |
| Inline styles preserve source text rather than exposing full CSSOM canonical serialization, shorthand expansion (a shorthand's longhands read back), indexed declarations and `CSS.supports`. D11 implements the longhand properties used by the chosen widgets; most of Modern Web slice 4's failing `-valid` WPT tests fail on exactly this (`0` for `0px`, `16/9` for `16 / 9`). Raised with Chris as its own libdom slice | Fidelity | M | a selected page reads normalized CSS text or shorthand state, or the libdom slice is scheduled | [DOM_D11.md](docs/design/pending/DOM_D11.md), libdom `style.c`; MODERN_WEB.md § Slice 4, as built |
| Arbitrary `clip-path` shapes require path clipping; D11 pays the rectangular legacy `clip` path | Feature | M | a page needs a clipped path to be readable | [POSITION.md](docs/design/completed/POSITION.md#booked-with-their-triggers) |
| **SVGElement is a name and a prototype, no members of its own**: `className` is Element's string where SVG makes it an `SVGAnimatedString` (INTERFACES_FORMS.md § Rulings, 4), and `ownerSVGElement` and `viewportElement` are absent. SVG's DOM is slice 6's (Quinn's, SVG.md), so P1a stopped at what Vue's mount asks (`instanceof SVGElement`) | Feature | S | slice 6, or a page reading `svg.className.baseVal` | libdom `node.c` (D_PROTO_SVG) |
| **A select has no indices of its own**: `select[0]` and `select[0] = option` (HTML's indexed getter and setter on HTMLSelectElement, the same as its `options`') are absent, since a select's wrapper is a node like any other and has no exotic behaviour; `select.options[0]` works | Fidelity | S (an exotic for the select kind, or a class of its own) | a page that indexes a select directly | libdom `options.c`, `node.c` |
| **A MessagePort cannot be transferred** (booked 2026-11-07 with MessageChannel). A port in a postMessage transfer list is a DataCloneError, where the standard moves it, entangled still, to the receiving side (`event.ports`): the window and its ports are one realm here, so the move is a renaming, but the clone record carries no port slot. WPT's close.any.html has two tests that transfer one; a scheduler never does | Feature | S (a port slot in the clone record, entanglement kept) | a page that hands a port through postMessage | libdom `message.c` (`port_member`), `clone.c` |
| **elementsFromPoint lists the hit and its ancestors, not every box under the point** (booked 2026-11-07 with R3a). The standard lists each element whose box is at the point in paint order, so two positioned boxes overlapping there both appear; yonder's hit provider answers the topmost hit (the pointer's own) and its flat-tree ancestors, which leaves out an element under the hit that is not its ancestor. elementFromPoint, the one pages mostly call (66 hosts to 24), is whole. And a frame's document answers null and an empty list: the provider hit-tests the page on screen and a page measured beside it, not a frame's viewport | Fidelity | M (a `flow_hit_all` collecting every box in paint order, RANGE.md § 8) | a page that asks what lies under an overlay | yonder `script_hit`, libflow `flow.c` (`hit_at`) |
| **A range's getClientRects walks every box once per Text node** (booked 2026-11-07 with R3b). yonder_text_rects walks the whole box tree for each Text node the range touches, and under collapsing white space flow_text_processed replays the node's text from its item's start twice per box, so `selectNodeContents(document.body).getClientRects()` costs Text nodes × boxes, and a long wrapped paragraph lines × bytes. Correct, and fast at the sizes pages ask about; a script can reach the worst of it cheaply | Performance | S (a node-to-boxes index and one replay per node) | a page that measures a large range | yonder `geometry.c` (`yonder_text_rects`), libflow `flow.c` (`flow_text_processed`) |
| **A range cannot sit in an Attr** (booked 2026-11-06 with RANGE.md R2). An Attr is a Node, and the standard lets `setStart(attr, 0)` and `selectNodeContents(attr)` make a range rooted at it (length 0); libhtml holds an attribute as its element's, not as a node, so a live range cannot point at one, and both throw NotSupportedError. What needs no such range answers as the standard says (an offset past 0 is IndexSizeError, `comparePoint` WrongDocumentError, `isPointInRange` and `intersectsNode` false, `insertNode` HierarchyRequestError); 15 of WPT's Range-attribute-nodes tests wait for it | Fidelity | M (an Attr node libhtml can hold, or a range kind of its own whose root is an attribute) | a page that puts a range in an attribute | libdom `range.c` `attr_answer` |
| **An element in no namespace or another one renders nothing** (booked 2026-11-07, when script could first make one). libflow boxes HTML elements and the SVG root and treats every other element's text as foreign, so `body.appendChild(document.createElementNS('urn:x', 'foo')).textContent = 'hi'` shows nothing where other browsers draw it inline | Fidelity | S (an inline box for an element no namespace claims) | a page that puts such an element in its body | libflow `style.c`, `boxes.c` (`foreign_text`) |
| **A fragment parsed in the context of an element in no namespace or another one parses as a body's** (booked 2026-11-07). libhtml's fragment parser takes HTML, SVG and MathML contexts only, so innerHTML, insertAdjacentHTML and createContextualFragment there use the binding's held body (`d_fragment_context`); the standard reads such markup as foreign content in the context's namespace (a tag that is no HTML breakout makes an element in that namespace) | Fidelity | M (foreign content in an arbitrary namespace in the fragment parser) | a page that sets innerHTML on such an element | libdom `mixin.c` `d_fragment_context`, libhtml `fragment.c` |
| **A prefixed custom element is not constructed by createElementNS** (booked 2026-11-07). | **Interface objects only for the binding's own prototypes.** Slice 0a names `Node`, `Element`, `HTMLElement` and the HTML elements that have a prototype of their own, and 0f's P1a added `Text`, `Comment` (with their constructors), `SVGElement`, `HTMLOptionElement` and `HTMLOptGroupElement`, and its page fixes `NodeList`, `HTMLCollection`, `HTMLOptionsCollection`, `DOMTokenList`, `DOMStringMap` and `HTMLTemplateElement`, and P1b `EventTarget` and `Window`; an element that shares HTMLElement's (`HTMLDivElement`, `HTMLCanvasElement`) and an SVG element that shares SVGElement's (`SVGSVGElement`) have none. Naming a broader prototype for them would make `instanceof` lie, so each needs a prototype of its own first. INTERFACES_FORMS.md ranks them and plans their phases (P6; the SVG subclasses are slice 6's) | Feature | S each | a page whose census line or WPT test needs one of them | libdom `node.c` (prototype kinds), `classic.c` `d_interfaces_install`; INTERFACES_FORMS.md |
| **The classic installers trust the engine's function constructors.** QuickJS's `JS_NewCFunction2`/`JS_NewCFunctionData` define `length` and `name` without reporting an out-of-memory there, leaving an exception pending under a function that looks made. The Modern Web table fails a row that leaves one pending; the classic installers before it do not ask, and the creation sweep has not met the case there | Robustness | XS | a creation-sweep cut that reaches one, or the next installer change in `classic.c`/`node.c` | libdom `core.c` `os64_dom_create`, `modern.c` `d_modern_install` |
| **QuickJS's parser still has out-of-memory paths it does not check.** Patches 0010 and 0011a made a failed emit, a failed constant-pool add, a failed closure-variable add or `arguments`/function-name add in `resolve_scope_var` and a failed buffer read by `get_lvalue` into out-of-memory exceptions. 0011b owes three more ((1), (2) and (4); 0017 paid (3)), in Fable's order: (1) `resolve_pseudo_var` answers -1 both for "no such variable" and for a failed add (`add_var_this` and its siblings), so `resolve_scope_var` resolves `this` elsewhere and the function computes a WRONG VALUE silently (a class method's `this.v` answered 0); its failure must be distinct and its callers must return it. (2) `add_eval_variables` ignores every failed closure-variable add. ~~(3) `resolve_labels` under a refused output buffer or relocation~~ **paid by patch 0017 (2026-11-01, Fable's review LIBJS_0017_REVIEW.md)**: an errored buffer has no room, so it holds exactly what was written before its error; a refused relocation marks the output errored instead of taking upstream's unsafe `fail:`, which is gone; nothing is patched after an error; and the refused output is what the definition's free walks. yonder's scripted-page sweep met it once streams.js moved its cuts; libjs's `with`, `eval_var`, `eval_arg` and `eval_with` sweeps are suite cases since. (4) `js_parse_skip_parens_token` swallows a failed atom and its eleven callers read the failure as "not that token", so the parse ends in a SyntaxError | Robustness | M + the libjs patch review | patch 0011b, after 0011a merges | `userland/libjs/upstream` (quickjs.c `resolve_pseudo_var`, `add_eval_variables`, `js_parse_skip_parens_token`); `tools/test_js_runtime_host.c` `compile_failure_pending` holds the sweeps that reach them |
| **An Error made by code compiled under memory pressure can read past a heap block.** P2a's first engine sweep refused every engine allocation in turn, in the DOM host's sanitized profile, across a whole `os64_js_run` of `Promise.reject(new Error('swept'));later=Promise.reject(2);queueMicrotask(()=>{throw 3});throw Error('own')` (the compile inside the cut); at one cut `new Error` reached `build_backtrace`, whose `dbuf_printf(" (%s", ...)` of the frame's filename read one byte past a 64-byte block (ASan heap-buffer-overflow in the port's `format`, under `js_error_constructor`). It looks like the compile left the function's debug filename broken, which is 0011b's family; the sweep that compiles first and cuts only the call does not reach it, and P2a's sweep was reshaped so. **Diagnosed 2026-11-04** when Intl's first-construction sweep met it too (and was reshaped the same way): the refused allocation is the script NAME's atom, the parse goes on with `JS_ATOM_NULL` as every function's `debug.filename`, and when the script later throws, `build_backtrace` prints atom 0, the engine's null entry, which has no characters and no terminator: the 64-byte block is that entry (`__JS_NewAtom`'s `JS_ATOM_NULL` struct). The cure is the parse refusing a failed name atom, or `build_backtrace` skipping a null filename | Robustness | S + the libjs patch review | patch 0011b, or a sweep of compile-and-run built to find it again | `userland/libjs/upstream` (quickjs.c `build_backtrace`, the compile's debug info), Fable's to rule; the sweep was `tools/test_dom_report.inc`'s first `dom_report_sweep_cases` |

What it takes: one HTTP/1.1 request with `Upgrade: websocket` through libfetch's connection (TLS included), then RFC 6455 framing on that socket — client frames masked with bytes from the random source Web Crypto already reads, ping/pong, fragmentation and close codes; on the script side an EventTarget with `send` (strings, `Blob`, `ArrayBuffer`), `close`, `readyState`, `bufferedAmount`, `binaryType`, and `open`/`message`/`error`/`close` events. Was: Promoted 2026-11-06: the Quick wins row "`setAttributeNS` and the other namespaced attribute verbs are absent" carries the priority and the census. libhtml's attribute verbs take a qualified name and no namespace (a set keeps the namespace of an attribute that is there and gives a new one none), so `setAttributeNS`, `getAttributeNS`, `removeAttributeNS` and `hasAttributeNS` are absent (WPT `dom/nodes`' attributes, attributes-namednodemap and Element-setAttributeNodeNS pages want them), and `setAttributeNode` of an Attr whose namespace the tree would lose (one from `createAttributeNS`, or an SVG element's `xlink:href` taken off and put back) is NotSupportedError | Feature | M | a page or a WPT directory worth namespaced attributes | libhtml `dom.c` (`os64_html_attr_change_t`), libpage `os64_page_node_set_attr`, libdom `attr.c`; LIBDOM.md § Attributes as nodes |
| **HTML reads an attribute by qualified name** (booked 2026-11-07 with the namespaced verbs). HTML's semantics read the attribute in no namespace; libdom's reflection and the cascade's attribute selectors read the first of a qualified name, in any namespace. Until `setAttributeNS` nothing but the parser made a namespaced attribute, and it gives no HTML element one; now a namespaced unprefixed `href` on an `a` reads as its reflected `href`, a namespaced `type` as an `input`'s reflected `type` (while its control state, libpage's, ignores it), and `[type=checkbox]` matches a namespaced `type`. | **A batch of attribute changes is reported by name, not by record** (booked 2026-11-07 with the namespaced verbs, from the cold read). `os64_html_set_attrs` applies a batch record by record, but `tell_attrs` replays it for the listener by name (`same_target`): on an element holding two attributes of one qualified name in two namespaces, a batch that removes one by name and sets the name again, or mixes a change by name with one by namespace on that name, reports the wrong old value and namespace to MutationObserver and attributeChangedCallback. ## Browser layout

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A script's paint-only style change takes a whole layout.** A script that writes `el.style.transform` or `opacity` (or the individual `translate`/`rotate`/`scale`) changes nothing any other box depends on, yet the change is a DOM mutation, so the next layout — the frame's, or the one a geometry read forces — rebuilds every box and lays the page out again (`flow_layout_restyled` copies unchanged styles but builds and lays out the whole tree; LAYOUT.md ruling 2), and the paint repaints the whole view. A JavaScript animation library writes those every frame from `requestAnimationFrame`, and a scroll-driven one reads a rect between writes: on the P5 (2026-11-10) a GSAP page's entry animation and its scroll-to-section ran at 40–100 ms a forced layout ("Script forced N layouts" in the status line), noticeably slow where its CSS and Web Animations would tick in place. **Census:** GSAP is in the kept scripts of 0 of 75 hosts in records 659–1572, and of about 8 (up to 10) of 85 in 1573–2405, when Chris began seeking out animation demos; scripts that write an inline `transform` or `opacity` at all are 48 of 75 and 63 of 85, mostly one-off writes (a carousel step, a menu's fade) that each cost one whole layout. **Cure:** after a script turn whose DOM changes are only `style` (or `class`) changes whose new computed styles differ from the standing tree's only in paint-only fields, restyle those elements alone, write the new values into their boxes in the standing tree as an animation tick does, and repaint their old and new ink — no layout; a geometry read then reads the standing tree. Anything that touches geometry takes today's whole path, which stays the fallback. Measure first with `--layout-profile` (restyle against box build against layout) | Performance | M (a short design note first: it changes how libflow's standing tree is updated) | a page Chris cares about made unusable rather than slow by its script animations; or animation-heavy pages becoming a target | libflow (the standing tree patched in place, beside the ticks: PILE3.md § Animations in place), yonder (`script_rebuild`, `layout_current`, the repaint's damage) |
| **Video, audio and embed have no stand-in.** With Modern Web enabled Canvas paints retained pixels and supported inline SVG paints its scene; disabled Canvas retains its stand-in and failure diagnostic. Video, audio and embed are counted as missing. | Fidelity | S each | a census where one is what a page was for | yonder `diag_elements`, `paint.c` |
| **Web-font pages discard geometry reuse after DOM changes.** **danlegt.com is the page to measure it on** (P5 record 1332, 2026-11-06): seven faces, and 1,274 layouts in 182 s at 83 ms each, more than its first layout cost, while its Scratch 2000 sprites move by style changes on timers; layout was 58% of the loop and the timers ran late 4,871 times. `layout_font_dependencies` advances the font resource revision whenever the DOM version changes and the cascade contains `@font-face`, because unicode-range selection reads characters across the document. This safely refuses grafting even when a mutation leaves the character set unchanged; incremental style copying still works. Compare the actual character dependency set (or verify equal hashes against the set), retaining font arrival, stylesheet and base-URL invalidation. Prove unchanged-character mutations can reuse geometry and changed selection still invalidates; obtain one P5 web-font-page graft reading with `reused > 0`. | Performance | M | booked before keyboard work; a web-font page with script-driven layout | Yonder `layout_font_dependencies`, `webfonts_chars`; [integration review](docs/design/pending/CANVAS_INTEGRATION_FABLE.md), finding 1 |
| **Canvas text allocates maximum outline scratch per draw.** `fillText` and `strokeText` reserve 4,096 commands (229,376 bytes) and free them per call, including short labels. Fifty calls reserve about 11 MiB cumulatively per frame, not simultaneously; the runtime cost has not been measured. Reuse bounded scratch or grow it to actual outline demand under the Canvas ledger. Do not assume a small fixed command allowance per glyph: outline complexity varies. Retain failure atomicity, the command cap and safe ownership across callbacks and teardown. | Performance | S-M | a text-heavy Canvas HUD, with allocation and frame measurements | `libdom/canvas_text.c` (`text_method`, `collect`); [integration review](docs/design/pending/CANVAS_INTEGRATION_FABLE.md), finding 2 |
| **Reading a gradient-valued Canvas style scans the DOM registry.** `fillStyle`/`strokeStyle` locate the gradient wrapper with a linear walk of retained `DValue` records. Provide a direct association with explicit binding/lifetime ownership, preserving object identity through save/restore and across contexts; wrapper collection remains separate work. A borrowed native user slot or DOM-owned lookup needs an ownership decision before implementation. | Performance | S | booked before keyboard work; repeated style reads amid many retained wrappers | `libdom/canvas.c` (`member`), `libcanvas/gradient.c`; [integration review](docs/design/pending/CANVAS_INTEGRATION_FABLE.md), finding 3 |
| **Canvas beyond the C6d geometry-query profile.** Rectangles, state save/restore, same-value dimension resets, ImageData, current paths, transforms, strokes/dashes, clipping, Porter–Duff compositing and retained presentation work. Path2D empty/string/copy construction and fill/stroke/clip overloads work. Path2D builder methods/addPath, roundRect, createPattern, path hit testing, DOMMatrix/getTransform and advanced blend modes remain absent. Retained Path2D wrappers share immutable SVG geometry and stay in the bounded DOM registry until teardown; high-churn path creation needs collectible wrappers. DOMPoint/DOMPointReadOnly and SVG isPointInFill work for the supported SVG geometry profile; Canvas isPointInPath/isPointInStroke, SVG stroke/length/CTM/BBox methods, per-shape SVG interface classes, DOMMatrix globals and structured cloning of geometry values remain absent. SVG graphics/geometry interface objects expose the implemented inheritance and fill query. Detached SVG fill queries throw InvalidStateError because the cascade has no detached-node style resolver; percentage geometry without a local SVG viewport is likewise refused. Canvas/image sources, smoothing, taint checks and PNG data-URL export work. Linear/radial/conic gradients, filled/stroked outline text, scheduled animation and shadows work. Shadow blur uses an interpolated three-box approximation with sigma capped at 64 device pixels; shadow composition scans the bitmap even for small draws. Exact Gaussian fidelity, compact shadow composition and Canvas filters remain expansion work. Creation supports transparent sRGB unorm8: opaque and wide-gamut contexts return null, and unsupported ImageData formats throw NotSupportedError. A refused resize drops the bitmap and exposes `isContextLost()` until a later successful resize, but does not dispatch contextlost/contextrestored events. ImageData allocations beyond the engine limit throw RangeError; the pinned WPT huge-constructor case expects IndexSizeError. | Feature / fidelity | C6, then profile expansion | the Canvas arc or a selected page needs an omitted profile/event | [CANVAS.md](docs/design/pending/CANVAS.md), `libdom/canvas.c`, `image_data.c`; pinned Canvas WPT expectations |
| **Canvas text and paint limits.** Text follows the shared Western profile; full bidi/complex shaping, font loading promises, small-caps/variation/stretch, letter/word spacing and extended TextMetrics editing methods are absent. Direction affects alignment and inherits HTML dir; CSS direction inheritance is not implemented. Baselines are synthesized from metrics. Viewport font-size units and newer CSS color syntax are unsupported. Gradient wrappers remain in the binding registry until teardown, bounded by its record quota and the Canvas owner ledger; repeated gradient construction can exhaust that quota even after script drops references. Add collectible gradient wrappers before supporting high-churn animation workloads. | Fidelity / lifetime | M by increment | C6 target capture or another page requires the omitted behavior | `libdom/canvas_text.c`, `canvas.c`, shared text engine; [C5 validation](docs/canvas-evidence/c5-validation.md) |

The captured page invokes chat before Scratch 2000 in one callback; an absent `IntersectionObserver` stopped it (it exists since 2026-11-04, INTERSECTION_OBSERVER.md; the capture has not been replayed since). Explicitly invoking the game initializer then reaches absent `Audio` in sound setup before wallet/ticket handlers are installed. The wall and selected rainbow cursor render in the captured replay; isolated floppy drawing/reveal works, but these do not establish full-site support. | Compatibility | Separate observer/media slices | a selected page requires these APIs; Danlegt is a diagnostic rather than the Canvas release gate | libdom, Yonder rendering loop; [C6 target checkpoint](docs/canvas-evidence/c6-target-checkpoint.md) |
| **Canvas image-source lifecycle and export limits.** Requests start when the document becomes the shown page; wrapped images receive load/error tasks. Responsive srcset/sizes/picture selection is shared by layout and Canvas ([I2 profile](docs/design/pending/IMAGE_MEMORY_I2.md)). Unwrapped markup image handlers, document-load dependencies and Image.decode remain outside the implemented image lifecycle. Metadata and Canvas share the completed display request ([I3 ownership](docs/design/pending/IMAGE_MEMORY_I3.md)); metadata creates no separate native bitmap. Reduced/refused rasters retain encoded backing when it fits and first drawing promotes synchronously. Whole arrivals retain encoded input through the first known layout decision when it fits; later reduction releases full owners not acquired by Canvas. Display resamples without backing use the normal request/cache path. Inactive request-owner eviction remains measurement-led work. The catalog retains at most 4096 entries and 4 MiB of URL strings. ImageBitmap, OffscreenCanvas, video and SVGImageElement sources are absent. SVG sources retain the secure-static subset and natural-size fallback. PNG export uses stored DEFLATE: exact bounded allocation but larger data URLs; decoding a large export can exceed existing URL/catalog limits. Smoothing quality hints share bilinear filtering. | Fidelity / performance | M by increment | image-heavy applications, export size or full HTML image loading | `libdom/canvas_image.c`, `libpng/encode.c`, yonder `canvas_source`; [C4 validation](docs/canvas-evidence/c4-validation.md) |

This is a rendering defect, separate from missing Canvas methods. Provably offscreen ordinary fills are culled before backend preparation; intersecting large coordinates and transforms still reach the bounded geometry backend's refusal limits; `2d.transformation.scale.large` and some large-curve/skew cases fail explicitly.
