| **Text at a tiny size draws as an 8x16 missing-glyph box.** A search page's timing marker, `<div style="position:absolute;font-size:1px;width:1px;height:1px">&nbsp;</div>`, draws a hollow box beside the results. Its run is laid out 8x16 at a 1px size: the bitmap face, the last fallback every family carries (libos64 `font_family.c` `entry_open`), took the characters, and the bitmap has no row for U+00A0, so `text.c` reports it missing and the missing-glyph box is drawn. Why the outline face did not serve a 1px NBSP is not yet pinned | Fidelity | S | another page whose 1px or invisible text shows as boxes | libos64 `font_family.c`, `text.c`; yonder `page_fonts` |
| **CSS system colours are not read** (CSS Color 4 § 6.2: `Canvas`, `CanvasText`, `Highlight`, `ButtonFace` and the rest). libgarb knows none of the names, so `color: highlight` and `outline: auto highlight` are refused whole and the page's fallback stands. Pre-existing — older than Modern Web slice 4, whose outline merely made it visible; one P5 page in the corpus replay writes it | Fidelity | S | a page whose look depends on one, or the next colour work in libgarb | libgarb `values.c` (colour names) |
| **A page's sheets have no bound together.** Each sheet's parse is held to `GARB_ARENA_MAX` (96 MiB) and its text to `GARB_SHEET_MAX`, and yonder keeps up to 64 sheets a page, @imports included (`SHEETS_MAX`), so a hostile page's parsed sheets can hold about 6 GiB. What a cascade reads of them is bounded (`GARB_CASCADE_READ_MAX`, 192 MiB a cascade); the parses are not. The cure is yonder counting its sheets' arenas against one page budget and refusing the rest, as a sheet past `SHEETS_MAX` is refused | Robustness | S | the next sheet work in yonder, or a page measured holding too much in its sheets | yonder `yonder.c` (`SHEETS_MAX`, the sheet table), `sheet.h` (`SHEET_RESERVE`); GARB.md § The cost |
| **Every cascade reads its sheets again.** What a cascade reads of a sheet — each rule's block, its selectors, its declarations' values — is its own and freed with it (GARB.md § The cost), so every layout that cascades, a resize or each layout a script measures by, reads every sheet again. A news page's 1 MB inline sheet is cascaded 11 times while it loads (its replay): reading is 0.04 s of each 0.1 s cascade on the host, half a second of the load, and a script that measures pictures one by one pays it per measurement. Read once at parse time, on the worker, it would be paid once a sheet and the sheet still never written by a cascade; the per-element matching is the other half of the time. Measured 2026-10-28: each block read is its own parse, and the project's own pull-request page has 22,415 rules (its scratch, one allocation a read until the row below was paid, is the cascade's now) | Cleanup | M | reached: a page whose loading is measured slow for it on the P5 | libgarb `cascade.c` (`add_rules`, `add_style_rule`); GARB.md § Booked before the first line |
| ~~**A declaration block's parse starts with no scratch and frees it when the block ends.**~~ **PAID 2026-10-30** (HEAP_BINS.md § Part A). One malloc and one free per rule per cascade, 22,000 of each on the project's own pull-request page, each malloc walking the heap's free list (§ Heap). A read's owner now keeps the scratch (`garb_parsed_t.scratch`): every block read borrows it and hands it back grown, and `garb_free` frees it, so a cascade starts one whatever its rule count (`test_garb_host.sh --scratch`, a cascade of 1,000 rules and of 2,000 each start one). A text parse still frees its own at the end, being read once. Reading sheets once (the row above) is independent: that would remove the parses, this removed the allocations the remaining parses made | Performance (PAID) | — | done | libgarb `parse.c` (`reparse`), `garb.h` (`garb_parsed_t.scratch`) |
| **The standing line's "laid out in" is the latest layout's, not the page's arrival.** `say_laid_out` is called for the arrival layout and again for every later layout of the page (a picture's size or a face coming in, a script's change), each overwriting the last, so the number read once the page has settled is a relayout's. Measuring the scratch fix on the P5 (2026-10-30), the pull-request page's line read 60 ms while its arrival layout, timed by eye, still took 3 to 4 s. The number to watch has to be read the instant the page appears, or by stopwatch. The line could keep the arrival's time and say the latest beside it | Observability | XS | the next layout measurement on the P5 | yonder `yonder.c` `say_laid_out` and its callers |
| **An `img` with no `src` is painted as a picture still coming.** libpage makes a page picture only of an `img` that names a source (`add_image`), but the glass paints every `img` box through the picture painter, and a box with no picture entry gets the "still coming" frame: a pale panel with a grey edge, at the size its `width`/`height` attributes and its styles gave it. HTML says an `img` with neither `src` nor `srcset` represents nothing, and Chrome keeps the box and paints nothing in it. The case that showed it (2026-10-28): a social news site's image gallery, whose second and third slides carry their addresses in `data-lazy-src` until the carousel's custom element fills them in, which never runs here, so two large pale boxes sit under the first slide's picture. The fix is a distinction in the painter, nothing drawn for a box whose node names no source; the box stays, as it does in Chrome | Fidelity | XS | reached | `yonder.c` `glass_image`, `pane_image`, `timed_image`; libpage `add_image` |
| ~~**The `-computed` WPT pages of Modern Web slice 4's six directories, slice 8c's `css/filter-effects/parsing` and 8d's `css/compositing/parsing`,** are excluded with "needs CSS.supports"~~ **PAID 2026-11-04** with `CSS.supports`: the exclusions are out and the eight directories gained 277 passes, losing none. css-logical's sixteen run and still fail, earlier than `CSS.supports`: the logical properties are not in getComputedStyle's longhand table, so `property in getComputedStyle(target)` is false (its new passes are its `-shorthand` pages). They were excluded because each page's helper (`css/support/computed-testcommon.js`) asks `CSS.supports` first, and a page failed whole without it (§ Classic browser scripting) | Fidelity | XS | — | `tools/wpt/expected/css/*/parsing.txt`, `css/css-sizing/aspect-ratio/parsing.txt`, `css/filter-effects/parsing.txt`, `css/compositing/parsing.txt` |
| **A closed `details` shows a child the page gives a `display`.** The rule that hides all but the first `summary` sets `display: none` before the author cascade runs, so an author `display` on a direct child (`.SelectMenu{display:flex}`) puts it back: the child is laid out, drawn and animated while the `details` is shut. HTML hides it whatever its `display` (the `::details-content` slot) | Fidelity | S | a page whose shut menus are `details` children with a display of their own (a code-hosting site's, 2026-11-05) | libflow `style.c` (`style_element`, the `details` rule) |
| **What bumps a page's form state on every frame is not known.** On a code-hosting site's pull-request page, moving the mouse starts a script's animation frames, and each one moves the page's form state (`os64_page_state_version`) with no control's value, choice or caption changed. The syncs it causes now mark only what changed (yonder `forms_sync_from_model`), so it costs a sync of every control a frame and no paint | Performance | S to find | a page whose frames each re-sync many controls | libpage `state.c` (`p_state_changed`'s callers) |
| **yonder has no instrument for where a frame's cost comes from, short of a diagnostic build.** The record's paint readings (`d`) say how many paints and how big, the biggest part marked and by what, and what keeps a page animating; finding WHY a page on a code-hosting site repainted 70% of the view sixty times a second (2026-11-05) still took five builds, each adding a counter (marks by the site that made them, syncs, placings, moves) and each installed on the P5 for one reading. Chris: "we're going to want to instrument Yonder. Hidden behind a flag like diagnostic." A second key in yonder.conf beside `diagnostics` (which names the page files' directory, and turns them off when it names none), `diagnostics_depth = deep` say, that keeps per-source mark counts, per-task-kind costs with the script that ran, and what each sync and placing touched, written with the readings, makes the next such hunt one install. Design first: what is counted always, what only under the flag, and what it costs a frame when on | Cleanup | M (a design note, then counters at the sites) | the next hunt that needs a build to measure | yonder `yonder.c` (`s_cost`, `diag_paint_reading`); YONDER_DIAGNOSTICS.md § What paints cost |
| ~~**A document whose sheets use `:has()` restyles whole on every change.**~~ **PAID 2026-11-08** (RELAYOUT.md § Selector invalidation for `:has()` and `:dir()`): a `:has()` in a subject marks the ancestors (and with `+`/`~` the preceding siblings) a change can turn, alone, and an anchored one remembers its answer on every element, so such a cascade tracks changes. What is left: **a bare anchored `:has()`** (nothing to test before it), **more than 64 anchors, or a `:has()` inside `:nth-child(… of S)`** still builds the cascade without a change reader, named on the profile's `layout cascade` line; **the marking is by ancestor chain**, an over-approximation of the per-selector invalidation sets other browsers keep, so with a `:has()` in a subject a mutation recomputes the winners of every ancestor of it | Performance | M (invalidation sets) | a page the `layout cascade` line shows refused for `anchors`, `bare anchor` or `counted has`, or whose profile shows the ancestor recomputes dominating | libgarb `select.c` `garb_selectors_anchors`, `cascade.c` `mark_has` |
| **`:dir()` ignores `dir=auto`.** An element with `dir=auto` takes its direction from its first strong character (HTML § The dir attribute); `dir_matches` reads only `ltr` and `rtl` up the inheriting ancestors, so text in a right-to-left script under `dir=auto` matches `:dir(ltr)`. Implementing it brings a dependency on descendants' text, which change tracking would then follow | Fidelity | S (the rule) + M (tracking it) | a page whose `dir=auto` content is styled by `:dir()` | libgarb `select.c` `dir_matches`, `garb_selectors_dependencies` |

## Filters (Modern Web slice 8c, FILTER.md, 2026-11-01 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A `backdrop-filter` inside a group on sheets is drawn unfiltered**: inside an opacity group laid through a matrix, a filtered group, a text clip, or under the element's own transform, what is behind it is the group's black and white sheets, not the page, so the pane refuses it and the paint counts it (`layer: drawn plain`). Filter Effects 2's Backdrop Root is the model to build | Fidelity | M | a frosted header that is also transformed or faded, shown unfrosted | yonder `yonder.c` (`pane_backdrop_filter`), `paint.c` (`backdrop_filtered`) |
| ~~**An inline-block's `backdrop-filter` is drawn but not joined to damage**~~ **PAID 2026-11-12** (the atom group): an atom with a backdrop filter is a stacked box now, so `flow_backdrop_damage` sees it | Fidelity | S | — | libflow `flow.c` (`public_frag`) |
| **A backdrop's margin is the window as last painted**: what it reads round its border box (its blur's reach) may hold something painted above it there — a neighbouring fixed bar — which a browser's backdrop would not see, since it reads only what is behind | Fidelity | S | a frosted bar whose edge picks up its neighbour's colour | yonder `yonder.c` (`pane_backdrop_filter`) |
| **An SVG filter reference draws its list unfiltered** (`filter: url(#f)`): read, named approximated (`css-value filter: url`), and the group painted as a plain one. No SVG filter primitives exist | Feature | L | a page whose look rests on an SVG filter | libflow `style.c` (`FLOW_FILTER_URL`), yonder `paint.c` (`filter_drawn`) |
| **A filter on a plain inline box is not drawn**, as its `opacity` is not (PILE3.md § Booked: no one layer to group). The INLINE-BLOCK half was PAID 2026-11-12 (the atom group, `public_frag`) | Fidelity | M | a filtered inline span a page shows unfiltered | libflow `internal.h` (`f_filtered`) |
| **A filter on a table's rows, row groups and columns makes no containing block**, as a transform on them does not | Fidelity | S | a page whose filtered row holds an absolute box | libflow `internal.h` (`f_contains_absolute`) |
| **Filter lists are not interpolated**: an animation of `filter` is discrete, the first value until halfway (Filter Effects 1's interpolation of filter lists is not built) | Fidelity | M | a page whose fade-to-grey or blur-in animation steps | libflow `motion.c` (`mix_prop`) |
| **An animated `filter` is laid out every frame, and a view-sized one repaints the whole view.** `filter` is in neither of motion.c's in-place classes (`repaints_in_place`, `ticks_in_place`), so `f_props_placed` names it "a value a layout places": each frame of the animation is a layout, and the box is marked whole. **danlegt.com** (P5 record 1332, 2026-11-06): `div.home-scratch-background` animates its filter and is as big as the view, so 1,843 of the page's 1,947 paints were the whole view ("a moving part as big as the view"), 24.5 ms a paint with gradients up to 128 ms, and 427 of its layouts were for animations. Other browsers treat `filter` as paint-only. **Cure:** `filter` (and `backdrop-filter`) tick in place, the damage the box plus the filter's reach (a blur's radius, a drop shadow's offset and blur), as `box-shadow` and `text-shadow` already do; then the whole-view mark is the filtered box alone, and the frame costs a paint | Performance | S | danlegt.com's fix day; any page with a filter animation on a large element | libflow `motion.c` (`repaints_in_place`, `ticks_in_place`, `f_motion_in_place`), yonder `paint.c` |
| **`e.style.filter` reads back as written, not canonical**: `blur(0)` stays `blur(0)` (WPT wants `blur(0px)`), `grayscale(300%)` is not held at `100%`, and a drop shadow's colour is not put first. libdom's specified value is the declaration's own text for every property, and `css/filter-effects/parsing` fails on each of these, for `filter` and `backdrop-filter` alike | Fidelity | M (a canonical serializer over libgarb's values, every property) | a script that compares a filter it set with what it reads | libdom `view.c` |
| **`calc()` in `hue-rotate`'s angle, and `sign()` and container units anywhere**, are refused, so the declaration is dropped | Fidelity | S | a page that writes one | libgarb `values.c` (`vc_angle`), the calc reader |
| **`color-interpolation-filters`, `flood-color`, `flood-opacity` and `lighting-color` are not read** (SVG filter properties, with no SVG filters to apply them to) | Feature | S | SVG filters | libgarb `props.c` |
| **What an ancestor's `overflow` clips is clipped before the filter, not after.** Filter Effects 1 filters an element's whole rendering and then clips the result; yonder cuts each box inside the filtered element to its ancestors' clips as it paints (`flow_box_doc_clip`), so a blurred child crossing an `overflow: hidden` edge is blurred from what the clip left, and fades a little early at that edge. The spread never passes the clip (the layer is cut to it, `layer_try`'s `outside`). The cure is the clip chain stopping at a filtered group, as it stops at a group laid through its matrix, and the clip applied when the layer is laid. Codex #445 round 1 | Fidelity | M | a page whose blurred or shadowed content meets a clipping parent's edge, and the edge's fade matters | libflow `flow.c` (`flow_box_doc_clip`), yonder `paint.c` (`layer_try`) |
| **A drop shadow's colour is not relit on a dark page**: the painter's relighting (paint.c's `relight`) moves the page's colours, and a filter's shadow colour is laid as written | Fidelity | XS | a dark page whose drop shadows show in the light page's colour | yonder `paint.c` (`group_close`), `filter.c` |

## Blending (Modern Web slice 8d, MIX_BLEND.md, 2026-11-01 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **An isolating ancestor does not hide the page from a blend inside it.** A blend's backdrop is the window, everything painted before it; the spec's is the content of its nearest isolated group (any ancestor that makes a stacking context, `isolation: isolate` among them). The two differ only where that ancestor leaves the page showing through itself under the blended element | Fidelity | M (an isolated group painted on transparent sheets of its own) | a blended element inside a transparent `isolation: isolate` wrapper, blending with the page behind it | yonder `paint.c` (`layer_try`), `yonder.c` (`pane_layer_close`) |
| **A blend inside a group on sheets, or through its own matrix, is laid normal**: an opacity group laid through a matrix, a filtered group, a text clip, or the element's own transform puts the layer onto a group's black and white sheets, and a blend is not linear in its backdrop, so the sheets could no longer recover their group. Counted (`layer: drawn plain`) | Fidelity | M | a rotated or faded blended element that a page shows unblended | yonder `yonder.c` (`pane_layer_close`) |
| **`mix-blend-mode` on a plain inline box is not drawn**, as its `opacity` is not (PILE3.md § Booked). The INLINE-BLOCK half, an inline logo among them, was PAID 2026-11-12 (the atom group, `public_frag`) | Fidelity | M | a word blended into a photo | libflow `internal.h` (`f_blended`) |
| **`background-blend-mode` is not read** (an element's background layers blended with each other): nothing in the census writes it | Feature | S | a page that writes it | libgarb `props.c` |
| **`plus-darker` is drawn as normal**, named approximated: its definition differs between Level 2 drafts and engines | Fidelity | XS | a page that writes it | yonder `paint.c` (`blend_of`), `filter.c` |

## The replay corpus (Modern Web slice 0b, 2026-10-21 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A replay lays text out in the harness's bitmap face.** The inputs a page is read with are its own, from its record (REPLAY.md § 3: the window's size and settings, the agent, the cookies a script could read, the clock and the zone), except those § 3 states the harness supplies, and this is one of them: its text is drawn in the host binding's bitmap face on clocks the replay moves itself, not the P5's fonts. The corpus is judged replay against replay, so this costs fidelity to the P5, not the regression check | Fidelity | S | a kept page whose replay differs from its P5 record because its text measures differently (a script that measures text, a layout that wraps) | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run` |
| **`screen` answers libdom's default on a replay.** The record keeps the view a page was laid out in, not the screen yonder ran on, and a replay never asks the desktop, so a script reading `screen.width` in a replay reads 1024 × 768 (`os64_dom_default_options`), and a sheet's or matchMedia's device-width, device-height and device-aspect-ratio answer that screen too, whatever display the page was kept on | Fidelity | S | a kept page whose MISSING lines, or whose layout, depend on the screen's size | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run` |
| **A replay's session history is a new window's.** `rp_run` starts every replay as a new way in a window with no history, and the record keeps none of the session the page was in, so `history.length` reads 1, `history.state` null, and no scroll is restored, for a page kept on a reload, Back or Forward, or with entries behind or ahead of it. Replays are compared with replays, so the check is unharmed; fidelity to the P5 is what it costs | Fidelity | S | a kept page reached by a reload, Back or Forward, or with entries around it, whose scripts read `history.length` or `history.state`, or whose replay differs by where it is scrolled | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run` |
| **`outerWidth` and `outerHeight` answer the harness's root on a replay.** yonder reads them from the window's frame as the compositor reports it (`frame_follow`, `g.frame_w`/`g.frame_h`), which a replay never sets, so they read the root's bounds as the harness lays them out around the record's view, not the frame the page was kept in; the record does not keep the frame | Fidelity | S | a kept page whose MISSING lines, or whose layout, depend on the window's outer size | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run`; yonder `view_read` |
| **Page storage starts empty on a replay.** The record keeps nothing of what the page's origin had stored, so a replay's `localStorage` and `sessionStorage` start empty and live in memory (the harness makes no store), and a page kept after it stored something (a consent flag, a cached answer) replays down the branch it took the first time it ever ran. Replays are compared with replays, so the check is unharmed; fidelity to the P5 is what it costs | Fidelity | S | a kept page whose record differs from its replay's by what it had stored | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run` |
| **A replay is always reached by its address.** `rp_run` starts every replay as a navigation by address, so `performance.navigation.type` reads 0 and the navigation entry `navigate` for a page kept on a reload or Back | Fidelity | S | a kept page reached by a reload or Back whose scripts read how it was reached | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run` |
| **`redirectCount` is 0 on a replay.** The head a replay feeds is built from the record, which keeps the final address but no count of the redirects that led there | Fidelity | S | a census page that reads `redirectCount` | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_head` |
| **A kept corpus holds the cookies a page's scripts could read.** The record's `cookies:` line is the jar's `document.cookie` string at the head, for the page's final address (never an HttpOnly cookie, which no script could read either), so a kept corpus is exactly as private as the browsing was, and its files are as readable as any other file on the disk: os64 has no file modes to write them closed with. `keep_pages` is a setting the person turns on knowing that | Privacy | S | the day the filesystem has modes: the record is written readable by its owner only | diag.c `yonder_diag_claim` / yonder.c `diag_cookies` |
| **keep_pages writes on the window's thread.** Each kept script or sheet is written whole, and the index again whole, when its job is reaped, so a page of large bundles pauses the window for the writes. A diagnostics setting, off by default | Performance | S | browsing with `keep_pages = on` on the P5 visibly hitching while a page's scripts arrive | yonder.c `diag_keep` / diag.c `yonder_diag_keep_resource` |
| **A page that goes somewhere of its own replays only as far as going.** A script setting `location`, a refresh or a form the page sends starts a navigation, and the replay stops there with `REPLAY incomplete navigation <url>`: the next page is not in this one's index, and the corpus keeps pages, not the chains between them. Such a page is never judged past that point | Fidelity | M | corpus pages that redirect by script often enough that their incomplete records hide what the slices change (a chain kept as the pages of one visit, each fed from its own record) | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_submit` / `rp_settle` |
| **A replay does not reproduce what a picture's or a font's fetch decides.** Neither is kept, and the replay never answers their jobs, so the lines yonder writes for what a fetch came to (MISSING `image`, FAILED `picture-fetch` and `font-fetch`) are not the page's on a replay's record: it says so (`REPLAY not reproduced: font-fetch image picture-fetch`), and the outcome and the diff count only the other kinds. A script that measures what a picture or a web font sizes measures it absent, so that page's other lines can differ from its P5 record too | Fidelity | M | a slice whose work is pictures or fonts wanting the corpus to judge it (keeping their bytes, as scripts and sheets are kept, and answering them from the index) | diag.c `yonder_diag_keep_resource` / `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `RP_NOT_REPRODUCED` |
| **A replay does not serve a page's modules.** A module's source is kept beside its page (a `module` line in the index, REPLAY.md § 4), so the census reads a module page's sources, but the module's fetch is the page's own request, which a replay never answers: a module page's replay waits on its graph and is UNFINISHED, as it was before modules were kept. Serving them means matching the module map's requests to their lines by token, as scripts are matched, and answering them in the kept order | Fidelity | M | a slice whose proof is a module page's replay (a GitHub page drawing; Intl's A2) | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_answers` / modules.c `kept` |
| **A form's reply replays as a GET.** The record keeps the page's head and bytes, not the request that fetched them, so a page that was the reply to a POST is fed back with nothing posted | Fidelity | S | a kept page from a form whose replay matters (its scripts read what was posted) | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` `rp_run` |
| **Three kept pages of 2026-10-22b replay unfinished since Modern Web slice 0c.** With jQuery booting, their scripts ask for scripts in an order and number the kept index does not hold, so a blocking request goes unanswered and the parse never ends (`REPLAY incomplete`): they are to be recorded again, as are the older pulls' pages that newly read incomplete | Fidelity | XS | the next corpus pull from the P5 | the kept corpus; MODERN_WEB.md § Slice 0c, as built |
| **A replay answers none of a page's script requests, and since slice 3 a module page waits on them.** keep_pages keeps a page's scripts and sheets, not what its fetch(), XMLHttpRequest or module fetches were answered, and the replay never answers a request job (`rp_answers`): a pending fetch never held a page up, but a module graph is a deferred script, so 85 of 2026-10-22b's 102 module pages replay unfinished at slice 3's head (96 in all, 13 at its base) | Tooling | M (request answers kept beside the scripts, read back by the replay, a request the index lacks a network error and the page incomplete) | the next corpus pull, which is the one that can show what modules did | `userland/apps/yonder/yonder.c` (keep_pages), `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` |
| **A failure's location names no inline script.** The record's FAILED line places a classic inline script's throw as `inline:<line>:<column>` (`frame_file`, scripts.c: every page inline script is compiled under one name), so a page with a dozen inline scripts of the same shape says which line of which script only by the column fitting; a P5 triage read script 3 for ten minutes when the throw was in script 8. The failing item's own name (`#inline-8`) is the right file name when the frame is the page's, and the kept index already numbers them the same way | Observability | XS | the next inline-script failure anyone has to find | yonder `scripts.c` (`frame_file`, `yonder_scripts_failure`) |
| **The record keeps none of the page's console.** `console.error` and `console.warn` go to yonder's stdout (`os64_js_install_output`, handle 1) and nowhere else, so a framework that catches its own exception — Next.js routing to `_error`, a React error boundary — leaves a clean record and a blank page: a stock-photo site's search died on `setAttributeNS` and the record showed only a late fetch of the error page; the cause took a terminal-launched yonder to see. Cure, under the same `diagnostics` folder that makes the record at all: keep the page's `error` and `warn` lines in the record, repeats collapsed with a count (a framework re-renders after each caught error and prints the same stack each time), capped with an "and N more" tail; stdout stays as it is | Observability | S | the next page a framework catches on its own | yonder `diag.c`, `scripts.c` (the console sink) |
| **The host replay runner gives the engine the 128 KiB floor, not the 2 MiB the P5 has.** `own_stack_bytes` (scripts.c) reads `/proc/self/maps` for os64's `[stack:<tid>]` row; on the host the page's thread has no named row, so `yonder_script_stack_limit(0)` answers `YONDER_SCRIPT_STACK_MIN`, and a React commit that fits on the P5 ends the host replay in `InternalError: stack overflow` (found 2026-11-06 replaying a kept page: the overflow left when the runner was handed 2 MiB by hand). Every host replay and census since SCRIPT_STACK.md landed has judged deep pages at the old limit. Cure: the runner states the stack the page's thread has (the host's thread attributes, or a size the harness passes), so the engine gets SCRIPT_STACK.md's budget on the host as on the P5; and the replay record says the budget it ran with | Fidelity (harness) | S | the next host replay of a script-heavy page | `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c`, yonder `scripts.c` (`own_stack_bytes`, `ensure_runtime`) |

## A script's requests (Modern Web slice 2, 2026-10-23 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **"Same site" has no public-suffix list behind it** (ORIGIN.md ruling 1). Two answers stand in. The CORS redirect-taint takes the same scheme and host for the same site (`way_origin_same_site`), finer than the real partition, so every answer it changes there is the restrictive one: more `Origin: null`, fewer preflight-cache hits. Fetch Metadata cannot use that, because calling a site's own subdomain `cross-site` is what a resource-isolation policy refuses; it asks `way_origin_site`, which says only what is certain and goes QUIET for two hosts that share their last two labels (sibling subdomains, and two sites under one public suffix): such a request carries no `Sec-Fetch-*` at all. In the kept corpus that is 11-21% of the https scripts and sheets a page asks for (W1's count, WIRE.md § 1 as built) | Fidelity | M (the PSL, which the jar needs too) | a redirect chain between sibling subdomains of one acceptance site answers `null` where Chrome answers the origin; a page whose CDN subdomain sends no Fetch Metadata where Chrome sends `same-site` | `userland/libway/origin.c` (`way_origin_same_site`, `way_origin_site`); [ORIGIN.md](docs/design/pending/ORIGIN.md); [WIRE.md](docs/design/pending/WIRE.md) § 1 |
| **SameSite is recorded and not enforced for script requests** (ORIGIN.md ruling 4): under `credentials: 'include'`, Lax and Strict cookies ride a cross-site fetch or XMLHttpRequest, where Chrome sends only `SameSite=None`. No more than a scripted `form.submit()` sends today, and the response stays unreadable without the server's `Allow-Credentials` | Security | S (the filter, in one function) once the PSL exists | the public-suffix list lands | `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` `hop_cookies`; the jar's SameSite debt |
| ~~**Script requests bypass the HTTP cache** (ORIGIN.md ruling 5).~~ **PAID 2026-10-30** (SCRIPT_CACHE.md). The cache keeps a reply's head and the request's lines for what it varies on, keys every entry by the page's site, the credentials and the address, and a hit goes through the live CORS check; a fetch's cache mode is honoured. A pull-request page's 207 module fetches a load were the trigger | Performance (PAID) | — | done | `userland/libway/cache.c`, `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` |
| **A script's request that varies on what the browser adds is not kept.** A reply whose `Vary` names a header libfetch or the agent's preset sends (User-Agent, Accept-Language, Cookie, Referer, `Sec-*`) is fetched every time: the fetch job knows only the request's own lines, so it cannot say what the reply was varied by (`request_value`) | Performance | S (the preset's and libfetch's lines, said to the job) | a site whose scripts or API answers vary on one of them, measured | `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` (`request_value`) |
| **A script's request reached through a redirect is not kept**, nor is a redirect: only a 200 at the address asked for, as CACHE.md's rule 1 keeps no redirect but a permanent one for a picture or a sheet | Performance | S | a site whose modules sit behind redirects | `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` (`cache_store`) |
| **A 304 that adds or changes `Vary` keeps the old recorded values** (Codex #425 rd5, booked at Chris's ruling that #425 was done). The merged head carries the 304's `Vary`, but the entry is saved with the request values recorded when it was first kept, so a reply first kept with no `Vary` and revalidated by a 304 saying `Vary: X-Test` then answers a request sending another `X-Test` without asking. The cure is in `cache_confirmed`: record the values again from the 304's `Vary` and the request that revalidated it (`way_vary_lines`), or drop the entry when they cannot be recorded | Correctness | S | a server whose revalidation varies on a header its first answer did not | `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` (`cache_confirmed`) |
| **What a script's request finds in the cache is not in the cache's counts.** Settings' hits and confirmations count `way_fetch_whole`'s alone (`way_cache_count` is libway's own); the page record says `kept` and `confirmed` on each request line instead | Observability | XS | Settings' counts read as the cache doing less than it does | `userland/libway/internal.h`, `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` |
| **A synchronous XMLHttpRequest's bound is its task's budget plus one of libfetch's waits.** The stop (the script task's own deadline) is asked before each wait, and a wait is not interrupted, so a silent server can hold the window up to the idle deadline (30 s) past the task's | Robustness | S (a wait sliced into stops, in libfetch) | a page whose synchronous request overruns its task by seconds | `userland/libfetch/fetch.c` (the waits), `userland/libdom/xhr.c` `sync_stop` |
| ~~**No streaming bodies**~~ **PAID 2026-11-03** (STREAMS.md § 10, S3): a body from the network reaches the page in pieces as it arrives, `response.body` grows while the page reads it, XHR's `progress` fires per piece, and TextDecoderStream and TextEncoderStream exist. Was: a body arrived whole, so `response.body` and XHR's `progress` saw it at once | Feature (PAID) | — | done | `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_pipe.c`, `userland/libdom/fetch.c`, `userland/libdom/xhr.c` |
| **No stream as a request body, no transferable streams, no `CompressionStream`** (STREAMS.md § 5, booked when the slice was cut). `new Request(url, {body: stream})` and `fetch` with `duplex: "half"` refuse a stream with a TypeError; `postMessage` cannot transfer a stream; `CompressionStream` and `DecompressionStream` do not exist (libgzip could serve them) | Feature | M each | a page that uploads a stream, hands one to a worker or frame, or compresses in the page | `userland/libdom/fetch.c` `d_body_extract`, `userland/libdom/streams.js` |
| ~~**An XMLHttpRequest keeps its body in the page's script memory**~~ **PAID 2026-11-04** (booked 2026-11-03 from the P5; XHR_BYTES.md). An XHR's received bytes live in the process's memory (`kept.c`), under the page's own body bound (`max_body_bytes`, 256 MiB), and a `blob` response refers to them, an `arraybuffer` one owns them, with no copy: eight 25 MiB downloads complete in a page whose script heap is 64 MiB, and a response past the bound fails as a network error while the page runs on (`dom_xhr_downloads_case`). A Blob the page makes keeps its bytes there too, and `send(blob)` refers to them (XHR_BYTES.md § 7, `dom_xhr_uploads_case`). What is left: **fetch()'s Response bodies** are still the heap's (their stream's chunks, and `arrayBuffer()`/`blob()` joined there); **fetch()'s request bodies, a FormData's multipart encoding and structured clone's copy of a Blob** are built in the heap, a kept Blob's bytes copied there; **a chunked body is joined at its end**, and the join's peak (pieces and block at once) counts against the bound, so chunked downloads fit half as many as declared ones; **a declared length is reserved whole at the head**, whatever then arrives; and **text and JSON** are decoded into the heap when read, as they must be | Fidelity | S each | a page that fetch()es more than its heap holds, uploads a large Blob through fetch() or a FormData, or streams large chunked downloads | libdom `kept.c`, `fetch.c`, `blob.c` |
| **A streamed body nobody will read is not given up.** The request holds the body's stream and its Response until the body's end, so a Response the page let go of still has its body delivered and queued, and a body past 16 MiB that nobody reads holds its pool worker, waiting at the pipe, until the page reads or leaves (STREAMS.md § 10). A page with as many such bodies as yonder has workers (12) that then waits on another request waits for good; browsers cancel a body whose Response was collected | Robustness | M (a weak hold: the request cancels when the stream is collected) | a page that drops large unread responses | `userland/libdom/fetch.c` `fetch_headed`, `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_pipe.c` |
| **A pipe hears its signal's abort as an event listener, not as an abort algorithm.** `pipeTo`'s `signal` is listened to with `addEventListener('abort')` (the captured one): a listener the page added earlier on the same signal runs first, and one that calls `stopImmediatePropagation()` keeps the pipe from ever shutting down (its source stays locked). DOM runs a signal's abort algorithms before its event, where no listener can reach them. Found by the S1 cold read | Fidelity | S (a door verb that adds an abort algorithm through abort.c's list) | a page that stops an abort event's propagation on a signal it also gave a pipe | `userland/libdom/streams.js` `readableStreamPipeTo`, `userland/libdom/abort.c` |
| **A clone stops hearing an abort when its original is collected.** The abort reaches a teed clone through the original Response, which holds the signal's algorithm; a page that clones, drops the original and reads the clone later has no abort reach once a collection finalizes the original (its finalizer settles it). Whether the clone's read is aborted then depends on when the collector ran. Found by the S1 cold read | Fidelity | S (the clone holds the signal and the source too) | a page that aborts a fetch whose original Response it dropped after cloning | `userland/libdom/fetch.c` `R_CLONE`, `response_finalizer` |
| **The streams' own promise reactions look up the page's species.** streams.js calls the engine's `then` (captured), and `then` itself asks `promise.constructor` and its `Symbol.species`, which a page may replace on `Promise.prototype`; the standard's PerformPromiseThen asks neither. The WPT pages that patch globals patch `then`, not those, and pass | Fidelity | S (an engine verb for PerformPromiseThen: a libjs patch) | a page that replaces `Promise.prototype.constructor` or `Promise[Symbol.species]` and uses streams | `userland/libdom/streams.js` `uponPromise`, `transformPromise` |
| **A `setItem` the engine runs out of memory during strands a quota-sized block.** Found when the Streams standard grew a realm by ~250 KB and pushed the DOM host's storage quota case past its fixture's 16 MiB engine cap: the case failed with "runtime limit exceeded", and the area's copy (5 MiB) was still allocated after the whole fixture was torn down | Robustness | S | a page near its memory limit that writes a large value to storage | `userland/libdom/storage.c`, `userland/libpage` |
| **The DOM host's allocation sweep cuts every allocation the Streams standard's load makes.** Installation's sweep went from ~3,200 cuts to ~28,400 when streams.js joined the realm, and the suite from ~2.5 minutes to over 10; the cuts inside the engine's run of the script prove libjs's failure latch more than libdom | Test cost | S (sweep the streams row's engine allocations at a stride) | the suite's time getting in the way | `tools/test_dom_cases.inc` `dom_allocation_cases` |
| **No document from a response**: `responseType = "document"` and `responseXML` answer null (the native XML shared-tree builder has no DOMParser/XHR integration) | Feature | M | a page that reads XML or HTML through XMLHttpRequest | `userland/libdom/xhr.c` |
| **Subresource Integrity is not verified**: `fetch()` rejects non-empty `integrity` with a network error; link and script integrity attributes are not enforced. Preserve fetch's fail-closed behavior while adding consistent verification across consumers; a preload must not claim its response passed a digest check | Feature | M (digest verification and consumer integration) | a page that requires integrity-checked subresources | `userland/libdom/fetch.c` `fetch_function`; Yonder sheet/script requests; `docs/design/pending/IMAGE_MEMORY.md` I0 |
| **`FormData(form)` reads the window's document's forms only**: libpage's model is the window's document, so a form in a document of its own answers no entries | Fidelity | S | a page that builds FormData from a form in a `createHTMLDocument` document | `userland/libdom/blob.c` `form_from_element`, `userland/libpage/submit.c` |
| **An address libpage's grammar refuses is a TypeError** to `fetch` and a SyntaxError to `open()` where the URL Standard parses it (userinfo, an unknown scheme), since the API parser is the page's link grammar | Fidelity | M (the grammar's) | a page that fetches an address with credentials or a custom scheme | `userland/libdom/url.c` `d_url_parse`; url.c's header names the grammar's gaps |
| **A redirect's `Referrer-Policy` is not applied to the request**: Fetch § 4.4 sets the request's referrer policy from a redirect response's `Referrer-Policy` header, and the job keeps the page's policy for the whole chain, so a later hop's `Origin: null` decision and its `Referer` differ from the standard's when a server tightens its policy mid-redirect. Fidelity, not safety | Fidelity | S (parse the header in the redirect step, one field into `way_request_t`) | a kept page whose redirect answers `Referrer-Policy` | `userland/libway/origin.c` `way_redirect_follow`, `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` |
| ~~**A released reader's `closed` is not marked handled, and a settled one is not replaced**~~ **PAID 2026-11-01** (STREAMS.md S1): streams.js is the standard's release, which marks `closed` handled through the captured `then`. Was: Streams' release rejects a pending `closed` with a TypeError (done) and marks it handled, and replaces an already-settled one with a promise rejected with a TypeError, also handled. The same holds for the `closed` an abort's error rejects (Streams' ReadableStreamError marks it handled too). Nothing the binding can reach marks a promise handled: QuickJS keeps the flag inside the engine, and calling `then` on it would run the page's `constructor` and species lookups. So since P2a a page that releases a reader without awaiting `closed` gets an `unhandledrejection` event and a FAILED line `unhandled rejection: TypeError: The reader was released`, and WPT's `response-stream-disturbed-1.any.html` ends its harness in ERROR on it (its tests count, by the runner's rule for a page's own error; MODERN_WEB.md § P2a, as built); and `closed` read after a release of a finished reader still answers resolved. A wrapper verb that forgot the rejection would not do: the script host would hear a later handler as a `rejectionhandled` | Fidelity (PAID) | — | done | `userland/libdom/streams.js` `readerGenericRelease` |
| ~~**A clone's body is not reached by its original's abort**~~ **PAID 2026-11-01** (STREAMS.md S1): a clone an abort can still reach tees the body's stream, and the abort errors the tee's source, so both see it. Was: `Response.clone()` copies the body, and only the original's is errored by an abort after the answer (the standard tees one stream) | Fidelity (PAID) | — | done | `userland/libdom/fetch.c` `R_CLONE`, `d_fetch_abort` |
| **`XMLHttpRequest.send(document)` sends the object's string as text, not the document**: XHR's send() serializes a Document (HTML or XML), UTF-8 encodes it and types it `text/html` or `application/xml` with `charset=UTF-8`; yonder has no serializer path to it from here, so a Document body is converted to a string like any other value (its `[object …]` name) and sent as `text/plain;charset=UTF-8` | Feature | S (the fragment serializer, whole-document) | a census page that sends a Document by XHR | `userland/libdom/xhr.c` `xhr_send`, `userland/libdom/fetch.c` `d_body_extract` |
| **A request the host would not start fires `error` inside `send()`**, synchronously, where a browser fires it from a later task | Fidelity | XS | a page that sets `onerror` after `send()` | `userland/libdom/xhr.c` `xhr_send` |
| **A request the pool has no slot for is a network error.** Every script request is one pool job, and the pool's table holds `OS64_WORK_MAX_JOBS` (256) at once, pictures and sheets among them; a request past it is refused at submit and the page sees a network error rather than a wait for its turn (the memory budget does queue) | Robustness | S (a queue of unsubmitted requests in yonder) | a page that keeps more than a couple of hundred requests in flight | `userland/apps/yonder/yonder.c` `script_request`, `userland/libos64/work.c` |
| **WebIDL operations are not enumerable**: `d_method` defines every operation non-enumerable, where WebIDL makes them enumerable (iterators' `next` is, `d_iterator_next`) | Fidelity | S (one flag, binding-wide, and its WPT rerun) | an idlharness page in a required set | `userland/libdom/core.c` `d_method` |
| **An attribute's getter has no name** (booked 2026-10-29, 0f's P8's oracle): WebIDL names an attribute's getter `get <name>` and its setter `set <name>`; `d_accessor` leaves both "". uievents' idlharness fails each attribute on it | Fidelity | S (binding-wide, as the row above, and its WPT rerun) | an idlharness page in a required set | `userland/libdom/core.c` `d_accessor` |
| **QuickJS does not refuse a Proxy whose `ownKeys` repeats a key**, so a `Headers` record built from such a Proxy is read rather than refused (WPT headers-record) | Fidelity | S (an engine patch) | the next libjs patch | `userland/libjs/upstream` (`js_proxy_get_own_property_names`) |
| **The WPT runner serves http on port 80**, so a `.sub.` test that compares an address carrying `{{ports[http][0]}}` reads `:80` where a URL drops it | Tooling | S | the next WPT runner change | `tools/test_yonder_wpt_host.c` `wpt_substitute` |

## ES modules (Modern Web slice 3, 2026-10-25 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**A script element fires no `load` and no `error`**, classic or module~~ **PAID 2026-11-06** (LOAD_EVENTS.md § 3.1): a module from a file fires `load` once it ran (a syntax error's too, since HTML rethrows it when the module runs), and a module whose graph failed fires `error`, inline or not, as does an import map with `src`. Was: HTML fires `load` at the element after its script ran and `error` when its fetch (a module's whole graph) failed, and yonder fired neither; most of WPT's module failures that are not workers were this (nested-imports, load-error-events, the instantiation and evaluation pages' `load` lines) | Fidelity | M | a page that waits on a script's `load` | `userland/apps/yonder/scripts.c` (`script_event`) |
| **A script a script inserts with `async = false` runs as an async one**, classic or module: HTML puts it on the list of scripts that run in insertion order as soon as possible, and yonder runs every script-inserted `src` script as it lands (WPT module/execorder's dynamic half); nor is `script.async` reflected, whose getter answers HTML's force-async flag | Fidelity | S (one more list, in order, and the flag) | a loader that inserts several dependent scripts with `async = false` | `userland/apps/yonder/scripts.c` `yonder_scripts_connected` |
| **A module's request says `Sec-Fetch-Dest: empty` and `Accept: */*`**, a `fetch()`'s, where Chrome says `script`: the page's request path (`os64_dom_request_t`) carries no destination | Wire | S (a destination field on the request, set by the map) | a server that refuses a module request by its Fetch Metadata | `userland/libdom/include/dom/dom.h`, `userland/apps/yonder/fetch_job.c` |
| **A script element's `crossorigin`, `integrity` and `referrerpolicy` do not reach its module's request**: a module is fetched with same-origin credentials, no integrity check and the page's policy whatever the element says (with `use-credentials`, no cookies go to another origin's module); an import map's `integrity` entries are read for their shape and dropped (libdom `importmap.c`) | Fidelity | S each | a page that sets one and depends on it | `userland/apps/yonder/scripts.c` `module_request` |
| **The module map is keyed without the fragment**: `./m.js#1` and `./m.js#2` are one module, and its `import.meta.url` has no fragment (WPT import-meta-url), where HTML keys by the whole URL | Fidelity | XS | a page that imports one module twice by fragment to get two instances | `userland/libos64/url.c` `os64_url_absolute` drops it |
| **A dynamic `import()` stalls the window while it fetches**: the engine's loader is synchronous, so a module no graph reached is fetched inline under the task's budget (the page's record says `synchronous`). A second consequence: the module is evaluated in the same microtask checkpoint as the classic script that imported it, while that script is still `document.currentScript`, where a browser's later task sees null (Codex #352 rd2) | Responsiveness | M (an asynchronous loader: prefetching is not possible, the argument being any expression) | a page whose lazy chunks make the window stutter on the P5 | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` `engine_source` |
| **A module's syntax error is compiled again for each run that reaches it**, so each script's `error` event carries a SyntaxError of its own, where HTML reports the one error it remembered (WPT's "exceptions are remembered") | Fidelity | S (keep the first error's value with its module) | a page that compares error objects across scripts | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` `MOD_BROKEN` |
| **A module's body can run twice after a failed resolve**: the engine unlists every module a failed run had not resolved (libjs CONTRACT.md § Modules), the loader then answers a later graph's import of one with a new copy compiled from the map's source, and the map's own handle still holds the old copy, so running that handle as an entry evaluates the module again, where HTML's map promises once. A `MOD_PENDING` module can be compiled twice under one name the same way (the loader answers its source before settle compiles it) | Fidelity | M (a libjs hook that lets the loader answer with the map's compiled module, not its source) | a page whose first module graph fails (a bare import, there being no import maps) and whose later scripts share its modules | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` `engine_source`, `userland/libjs/runtime/runtime.c` `module_load` |
| **A task's second failure reaches no FAILED line**: a task's outcome holds one failure, and libdom and yonder keep the first (`d_outcome_fold`, yonder's task paths), so a second exception in one task (a second listener that throws, a job that throws after the script did) and a second module's late failure surfaced in one task are heard by the window's `error` event and are on no record line and no status line. A second unhandled rejection is told (`options.failed`), which is the shape a second exception could take. Found by the cold read of MODULE_FAILURES.md's build, for late module failures; the exceptions' half is older | Fidelity | S (tell a task's later failures through `options.failed`, as its later rejections are) | a page whose second failure in one task is the one that explains it | libdom `report.c` (`d_errors_fire`, `unhandled`), `event.c` `d_outcome_fold` |
| ~~**A module's late failure goes unheard when an `import()` of it is waiting**~~ **PAID 2026-11-07** (MODULE_FAILURES.md): libjs watches each module still evaluating when its run returns with a handler of its own (`module_watch`, through the engine's `then` read at construction), so a handled evaluation is heard too, and a failure after the run returned is owed to the outcome of the call whose jobs surfaced it (`module_owed_fold`): the record's FAILED line and the status line, as for any script. Found broader than this row: with no `import()` at all, no late module failure reached an outcome, since libdom dropped it when no window `error` listener was attached, which is how a social network's logged-out page went blank without a word. Was: a module paused at a top-level `await` that another script `import()`s before it rejects has a handler on its evaluation promise, so the engine never tells the rejection tracker, and the module script's error never reaches the window | Fidelity | S | a module with a top-level `await` that rejects after another script imported it | `userland/libjs/runtime/runtime.c` `module_watch`, `module_owed_fold` |
| **An entry whose module the loader fetched inline has no handle**, so its run reports "no memory to run the module" where HTML runs the already-evaluated module as nothing | Fidelity | XS (a `MOD_LOADED` entry runs a one-line importer, as a broken one does) | a dynamic `import()` of an entry's own URL while that entry's fetch is out | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` `yonder_modules_run` |
| **A second entry naming a module whose run threw reports nothing**: `yonder_modules_run` answers OK for a module already run, where HTML rethrows the remembered error at each script that names it | Fidelity | XS (keep the first run's failure and answer it again) | two `<script type=module src>` naming one failing module | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` `yonder_modules_run` |
| **A module the loader fetched inline (a dynamic `import()`, or one the engine let go of) has the request's URL as `import.meta.url`**, where the map's async path, and HTML, use the response's URL after redirects | Fidelity | XS (the loader's `source` hands back the slot's base) | a dynamically imported module served through a redirect that reads `import.meta.url` | `userland/libjs/runtime/runtime.c` `module_load`, `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` `engine_source` |
| **Two scope keys in one import map that normalize to the same URL are combined**: `"/s/"` and `"https://example.com/s/"` on that page give one scope holding both maps' entries, where HTML's "sort and normalize scopes" lets the later replace the earlier whole (Codex #366). libdom's walk hands entries, not scope boundaries, so yonder cannot tell a scope written twice from one written once | Fidelity | S (a scope-begins call in the walk, which empties the scope) | a page whose import map spells one scope two ways | `userland/libdom/importmap.c` `scope_entries`, `userland/apps/yonder/importmap.c` `yonder_import_map_add` |
| **With modern scripting off, a module or import map script a verb connects is marked already started**, where HTML returns before that mark for a type the UA does not run, so an element whose `type` later becomes classic never runs | Fidelity | XS | a page with modern off that retypes a connected module script | `userland/libdom/window.c` `d_script_connected` |
| **A module preload is a parse-time look only, and says nothing back**: `yonder_scripts_preload` walks the tree at the stream's looks for its sheets, so a `<link rel=modulepreload>` a script inserts after the parse is not preloaded (its module is fetched when something imports it, as without the hint), and a preload fires no `load` or `error` at its link, nor does its `crossorigin`, `integrity` or `referrerpolicy` reach the request (the script element's row above, for links). WPT's `preload/modulepreload*` pages are mostly script-inserted links waiting on `load` | Fidelity | S (libdom reporting a connected preload link, as it reports a connected script; the events with the script element's) | a page that inserts preloads after its parse and measures them, or waits on their `load` | `userland/apps/yonder/scripts.c` `yonder_scripts_preload` |
| **`relList` is a link's alone**: HTML gives `<a>`, `<area>` and `<form>` one too (whose supported tokens are `noreferrer`, `noopener`, `opener`), and yonder reflects none of them | Fidelity | XS | a page that feature-detects `noopener` through `a.relList.supports` | `userland/libdom/mixin.c` |
| **A module refused by a full work pool fails instead of waiting**: `fetch_start` (modules.c) submits every module a graph names at once, and a submit the pool refuses for a full table fails the module ("the module could not be fetched", no request made). The table was raised from 256 to 1024 slots on 2026-10-31, when a corpus page's 227 modules beside its other jobs lost 20 of them; a page past about a thousand would lose them again. Pictures and web fonts cap how many are in flight and queue the rest (`PICTURES_AT_ONCE`, `WEBFONTS_AT_ONCE`) | Fidelity | S (a modules-at-once cap, the rest waiting in the map and started as answers land) | a page that imports more than about a thousand modules | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c` (`fetch_start`) |
| **No `import.meta.resolve`, JSON or CSS modules (import attributes), or module workers** | Feature | M each | a site that uses one | `userland/apps/yonder/modules.c`, libdom |

## The bytecode cache (Modern Web slice 7, BYTECODE_CACHE.md, 2026-10-30 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~**A module is compiled every visit** (7b)~~ **PAID 2026-10-30** (SCRIPT_CACHE.md § 8, § 11): a module whose answer the HTTP cache holds brings its blob, read by its fetch job beside the entry under the module's tag (its body's FNV-1a and length), and `os64_js_module_compile_bytecode` reads it when the blob was written for that module's name; a compile is kept beside the entry by the write job a classic script's uses | Performance (PAID) | — | done | yonder `modules.c` (`try_compile`), `fetch_job.c` (`module_bytecode`); libjs `os64_js_module_compile_bytecode` |
| **A dynamic `import()`'s module is compiled every visit**: the engine's own loader compiles it from the text the map's `source` answers, which carries no blob, and its fetch (a synchronous one, inside the task) is not flagged as a module's | Performance | S | a page measured slow on a dynamic import | yonder `modules.c` (`engine_source`), `scripts.c` (`module_run_now`) |
| **An inline script is compiled every visit.** A page is not cached, so an inline script has no entry to keep a companion beside, and one keyed by its text alone would be a second cache with a cap of its own | Performance | M | a page measured slow on a large inline script | yonder `scripts.c` (`run_item`) |
| **A script or module past 8 MiB is refused before it compiles** (`SCRIPT_SOURCE_MAX`, the source limit a page's runtime is made with; it was 4 MiB until a code-hosting site's entry module grew to 5.5 MiB on 2026-11-05), so YouTube's 10.6 MB bundle never runs, cached or not. The page's heap is a setting since then (`yonder.conf`'s `script_memory_mb`, 512 MiB by default; each record's `script heap` line says what a page held), and the bytecode cap follows the source limit (`SCRIPT_BYTECODE_MAX`, four times it) | Compatibility | S (the numbers) | a page past 8 MiB that should run | yonder `bytecode.h` |
| **A record does not say which script heap or document budget its page ran with.** Neither `script_memory_mb` nor `page_memory_mb` is among the record's settings (`kDiagSettings`), so a write does not compare them and a replay runs every page at yonder's defaults (REPLAY.md § 3), whatever the page was kept under | Robustness | S | a page kept under a heap other than the default, or the next change to the record's settings | yonder `yonder.c` `kDiagSettings`; `tools/test_yonder_replay_host.c` |

## Custom elements (CUSTOM_ELEMENTS.md, 2026-10-29 — booked the day it was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The parser upgrades an element it makes after its definition exists, where HTML constructs it bare** (decision 6): its constructor runs with its attributes already present and the element connected, and `attributeChangedCallback` fires once for each observed attribute. A constructor that reads `this.getAttribute` in its body, or counts its callbacks, can tell. The upgrade runs before any later script (yonder runs what the parser left owed after each parser call) | Fidelity | M (the parser asking libdom to construct, which means running a page's code inside a parser call) | a page whose element's constructor depends on running before its attributes are set | `userland/libdom/custom.c`, `userland/apps/yonder/yonder.c` `stream_reactions` |
| **No `ElementInternals` and no form-associated custom elements**: `attachInternals` is undefined, and a definition's `formAssociated` and its four form callbacks are read as the standard reads them and kept by nothing, so such an element takes no part in its form | Feature | M | a site whose custom controls submit with a form, or that calls `attachInternals` | `userland/libdom/custom.c` `read_definition` |
| **No customized built-in elements**: `define`'s `extends` is a NotSupportedError, as in Safari, and `is=` is ignored | Feature | M | a site that relies on `is=` with no fallback | `userland/libdom/custom.c` `define` |
| **A "replace all" is several mutation records, not one**: `textContent`, `innerHTML` and `replaceChildren` reach libpage's `replace_children`, which inserts the new content and then removes each old child, so an observer gets an insertion record and a removal record for each child where DOM queues one record with both lists. Every change is still seen. One record needs a libhtml verb that tells it, and a record whose removed nodes are a list (they no longer lie together as siblings), which cut a's runs cannot say | Fidelity | M (a libhtml replace-children verb and an array form of the listener's removed nodes) | an observer that counts records, or WPT's textContent, replaceChildren and inner-outer lines | `userland/libpage/state.c` `tree_change`, libhtml `os64_html_change_t` |
| **A MutationObserver's own record lives until the page goes**: its object is collected when nothing observes, but the C state beside it (a few dozen bytes) is freed only at teardown | Memory | XS | a page that makes observers by the thousand | `userland/libdom/observer.c` |
| **The binding's operations are not enumerable**: `d_method` and `d_method_named` define every method writable and configurable but not enumerable, where WebIDL makes an operation all three. Binding-wide, not custom elements' alone (found writing their tests). idlharness checks it; a page almost never enumerates a prototype | Fidelity | S (one flag in the two definers, and the DOM host's chain-shape and idlharness expectations moved with it) | idlharness lines worth earning, or a page that walks a prototype's own keys | `userland/libdom/core.c` `d_method`, `d_method_named` |

It joins `tools/intl_captures/` as the gate once they agree, since the check excludes nothing. | **The Collator does not normalize**: strings that differ only in the order of two or more stacked combining marks ("ä" plus a dot below, against "a", a dot below, a diaeresis) compare unequal; one mark against its precomposed letter compares equal, since the root gives both the same elements. test262's two canonical-equivalence tests (Collator's, and localeCompare's 15.5.4.9_CE) are excluded for it | Fidelity | S (NFD and canonical reordering for the table's ranges, generated) | a page comparing text typed with stacked marks | `userland/libjs/intl/intl.js` (`collationUnit`) |

## Web Animations (WEB_ANIMATIONS.md slices A and B, 2026-11-09 — booked the day each was built)

| Debt | Class | Size | Trigger | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A script keyframe's `var()` is not animated, and neither is a custom property (`--x`) a keyframe sets**: `getKeyframes` gives both back as the page wrote them (slice B), but neither reaches libflow. libgarb substitutes `var()` in an element's `@keyframes` inside the cascade; a script's keyframes arrive from outside it, so `garb_read_declaration` answers `GARB_DECL_HELD` and the value is left out of the effect as a value that does not parse is. The cure is a libgarb entry that substitutes a list of sets against an element's custom properties | Fidelity | S | a page that animates `var(--x)` from script (libraries hand `animate()` resolved values) | libdom `webanimation.c` (`data_make`), libgarb |
| ~~**`composite` and `iterationComposite` are kept and not applied**~~ **PAID 2026-11-09 (slice B2)**: libdom resolves each keyframe's composite (`auto` takes the effect's) and libflow makes each endpoint of an interval in Web Animations 2's order — accumulated with the final keyframe's value once for each finished iteration (`accumulate_onto`), then met with the value underneath where it adds or accumulates (`add_prop`) — before the interval mixes them. What it does not reach is booked in the rows below | Feature | — | PAID | libdom `webanimation.c` (`effect_composites`); libflow `motion.c` (`final_value`, `accumulate_onto`, `add_prop`) |
| **`add` and `accumulate` meet only the kinds that add**: colours, lengths, the numbers (font size and weight, opacity, letter spacing, outline width and offset, z-index) and transforms. Every other kind a keyframe sets (shadows, background lists, radii, gaps, line-height, filters and the rest) replaces, the keyframe's value as it stands | Fidelity | S | a page that adds a shadow or a radius from script | libflow `motion.c` (`add_prop`'s `default:`) |
| **A transform list that does not pair function by function accumulates as a replace**: Web Animations accumulates such lists by decomposing both into matrices and accumulating their parts; libflow takes the keyframe's list, and the same for the individual `rotate` accumulated onto a rotation about another axis. `add` concatenates lists and composes rotations, as the spec does | Fidelity | S | a page that accumulates a `rotate()` onto a `matrix()` | libflow `motion.c` (`add_transforms`, `add_individual`) |
| **An opacity keyframe is held to [0, 1] as it is read**, before it is composited, where the spec composites the value given and clamps the result: `add` of `-0.5` onto `0.8` gives 0.8, where the spec gives 0.3. WPT's `clamping-001.html` has four tentative cases on it | Fidelity | XS | a page that adds an out-of-range opacity | libgarb (the keyframe's read); `tools/wpt/expected/web-animations.txt` |
| **CSS `animation-composition` is not read**: a CSS animation always replaces | Feature | S | a page whose CSS animations add | libgarb, libflow `style.c` (CSS frames' `GARB_COMPOSITE_REPLACE`) |
| **A CSSAnimation's effect has no keyframes of its own**: `getKeyframes()` answers `[]`; `setKeyframes()` and `updateTiming()` change the object and not what is drawn, which stays the style's `@keyframes` at the style's timing; `commitStyles()` writes nothing; and an effect set on it is not drawn. Its clock (pause, play, seek, rate, finish, cancel) is the whole of what a script drives | Fidelity | M (the host hands the style's keyframes to the effect) | a page that reads or retimes a CSS animation from script | libdom `webanimation.c` (`css_wrap`) |
| **The CSS animation events are worked out once a step, from the host's animations as they stand**: an `animationcancel`'s `elapsedTime` is the active time when the step noticed the cancel (within a frame of a script's cancel; for a CSS animation that leaves the style, at the step after the layout that dropped it); when a listener arrives, an animation already there at the step before is taken where it stands, so a boundary it crossed between that step and the listener's first (its start, when it began just before the listener was added) goes unheard; a paused animation's events are scheduled at the step's time; and one at a rate other than 1 is watched every frame while a listener waits (one at rate 1 wakes the step only at its boundaries) | Fidelity | XS | a page that times these to the millisecond | libdom `webanimation.c` (`css_events`, `css_due`) |
| **A page's CSS animations start when it is first laid out to be shown**, so a script that asks before then — an inline script during load, while the page is laid out only to be measured — finds none: `getAnimations()` answers its script animations alone and no CSS animation event can come yet. Other browsers start a CSS animation at the first style resolution. This is why WPT's `css/css-animations` CSSAnimation pages fail nearly whole (they ask at load); libdom's host tests prove the CSSAnimation itself | Fidelity | M (a coming page's measured layouts build a timeline the shown page then takes over) | a page that reads its CSS animations from a load-time script, or WPT's CSSAnimation pages wanted | yonder `yonder.c` (`css_animations_of`, `page_timeline`, the measured layout's NULL timeline); `tools/wpt/expected/css/css-animations.txt` |
| **`commitStyles()` writes the target's computed value**, which is the effect stack with every animation in it, where the spec's commitStyles() writes the stack up to and including the one committed: a later animation on the same property commits its value instead, and a removed animation's own value is not what it commits (its effect is no longer in the stack). Libraries commit their newest animation, where the two agree | Fidelity | S (a computed style of the stack cut at one animation: a libflow entry beside the computed provider) | a page that commits an older animation of a property a newer one also sets | libdom `webanimation.c` (`commit_styles`) |
| **There is one timeline**: `Animation.timeline` cannot be set, `new DocumentTimeline()` is refused, and `new Animation(effect, timeline)` takes the document's timeline or null (an animation made with null never starts: its pending play waits for a time it will not have) | Feature | S | WPT's `DocumentTimeline/constructor.html` and the timeline setter's tests; a page that makes its own timeline | libdom `webanimation.c` (`animation_construct`) |
| **`getKeyframes` gives each value as the page wrote it**, where Chrome gives the declaration's serialisation (`rgb(1,2,3)` reads back `rgb(1, 2, 3)`); and a keyframe offset given as a `calc()` string (Web Animations 2) is refused, as a number outside [0, 1] is | Fidelity | S | WPT's `getKeyframes.html` serialisation test and the calc() offsets of `constructor.html` and `setKeyframes.html` | libdom `webanimation.c` (`get_keyframes`, `offset_of`) |
| **WPT's `getAnimations.html` is excluded**: its "Triggers a style change event" test waits for a CSS transition's `transitionrun`, and since slice B's constructors let it reach that wait the page times out whole, so its passes cannot count. libdom's own tests hold `getAnimations` meanwhile | Fidelity | XS (falls out of CSS transitions) | CSS transitions (PILE3.md § Transitions) | `tools/wpt/expected/web-animations.txt` |
| **Scroll-driven timelines are absent**: `ScrollTimeline`, `ViewTimeline` and `animation-timeline` (12 census hosts). Every animation runs on the document timeline | Feature | L | a page whose animation follows its scroll | WEB_ANIMATIONS.md § 4 |
| **A keyframes sequence is walked to its end before any keyframe is read**, where § 6.6.3 processes each as it is yielded: an iterator that hands out one object and changes it between yields gives every keyframe its last values (Codex #515 round 3, booked: a page has to build that) | Fidelity | S | a generator that reuses the object it yields | libdom `webanimation.c` (`process_keyframes`, `d_sequence`) |

| **The update step runs a task every frame while a script animation or a CSSAnimation runs**, whether or not anything listens for its events or reads its promises, so the finish can be noticed on time.
